43	\begin{figure*}[t]
44	\centering
45	\includegraphics[width=0.7\textwidth]{figures/fig_method_overview.pdf}
46	\caption{Experimental framework. For each architecture, we compare the pure-recurrent baseline against its hybrid (+attention) variant, classifying every evaluation example as helped, hurt, or unchanged. Cross-architecture overlap of these per-example effect sets is measured via pairwise Jaccard similarity ($J \approx 0.10$), revealing architecture-specific sensitivity patterns.}
47	\label{fig:method_overview}
48	\end{figure*}
49	
50	\subsection{Convergence Metrics}\label{ssec:convergence_metrics}
51	
52	We quantify cross-architecture agreement as a function of attention ratio. For each benchmark and attention ratio, we compute the cross-architecture standard deviation $\sigma$ over the three architectures' accuracy scores. To measure the relationship between attention and convergence, we compute the Spearman rank correlation $\rho$ between attention ratio rank and $\sigma$ across the five attention configurations ($n = 5$). A negative $\rho$ indicates that higher attention ratios are associated with lower cross-architecture variance.

70	\subsection{Per-Example Decomposition}\label{ssec:per_example_decomposition}
71	
72	We decompose performance changes at the individual-example level on four benchmarks spanning three task categories: LAMBADA (5{,}153 examples; retrieval), HellaSwag (10{,}042; reasoning), PIQA (1{,}838; reasoning, two-choice), and ARC-Easy (2{,}376; mixed). HellaSwag uses normalized accuracy (acc\_norm) as the per-example correctness criterion. Supplementary analyses on BoolQ (3{,}270) and WinoGrande (1{,}267) appear in Appendix~\ref{app:boolq} and \ref{app:winogrande}. For each architecture, we compare the pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid (25\% attention), which maximizes the recurrent--hybrid contrast; \S\ref{ssec:per_example} confirms that $\alpha$ is stable across intermediate ratios (variation $< 0.03$). Each example is classified as:
73	
74	\begin{itemize}
75	    \item \textbf{Helped}: correct in the hybrid but incorrect in the pure model.
76	    \item \textbf{Hurt}: correct in the pure model but incorrect in the hybrid.
77	    \item \textbf{Unchanged}: both models agree (both correct or both incorrect).
78	\end{itemize}
79

40	
41	All evaluations use lm-evaluation-harness v0.4.x \citep{gao2024lmeval} with default prompts and greedy decoding; accuracy serves as the primary metric for all benchmarks. Bootstrap analyses use random seed 42 throughout.
42	
43	\subsection{Convergence Metrics}\label{ssec:convergence_metrics}
44	
45	We quantify cross-architecture agreement as a function of attention ratio. For each benchmark and attention ratio, we compute the cross-architecture standard deviation $\sigma$ over the three architectures' accuracy scores. To measure the relationship between attention and convergence, we compute the Spearman rank correlation $\rho$ between attention ratio rank and $\sigma$ across the five attention configurations ($n = 5$). A negative $\rho$ indicates that higher attention ratios are associated with lower cross-architecture variance.
46	
47	We also report the convergence ratio $\sigma_{\text{pure}} / \sigma_{\text{h3-1}}$, defined as the standard deviation under the pure-recurrent configuration divided by that under the highest attention ratio (25\%). Values greater than 1 indicate that higher attention ratios are associated with lower cross-architecture variance.
48	
49	\begin{figure}[t]
50	\centering
51	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_per_example_bars.pdf}
52	\caption{Per-example effects of adding attention (pure $\to$ h3-1) on LAMBADA (1.3B). Helped: correct only with attention; hurt: correct only without. HGRN2 shows the only net positive effect; overlap is minimal ($\bar{J} \leq 0.10$, all $p < .0001$).}
53	\label{fig:per_example_bars}
54	\end{figure}
55	
56	\begin{figure*}[t]
57	\centering
58	\includegraphics[width=0.75\textwidth]{figures/fig3_alpha_routing.pdf}
59	\caption{(a) Per-example ambiguity $\alpha$ varies $2\times$ across task types. (b) Confidence-based routing gains track $\alpha$: effective when multiplicity is high (LAMBADA) but counterproductive when low (PIQA). 1.3B, h3-1.}
60	\label{fig:alpha_routing}
61	\end{figure*}

40	
41	All evaluations use lm-evaluation-harness v0.4.x \citep{gao2024lmeval} with default prompts and greedy decoding; accuracy serves as the primary metric for all benchmarks. Bootstrap analyses use random seed 42 throughout.
42	
43	\subsection{Convergence Metrics}\label{ssec:convergence_metrics}
44	
45	We quantify cross-architecture agreement as a function of attention ratio. For each benchmark and attention ratio, we compute the cross-architecture standard deviation $\sigma$ over the three architectures' accuracy scores. To measure the relationship between attention and convergence, we compute the Spearman rank correlation $\rho$ between attention ratio rank and $\sigma$ across the five attention configurations ($n = 5$). A negative $\rho$ indicates that higher attention ratios are associated with lower cross-architecture variance.
46	
47	We also report the convergence ratio $\sigma_{\text{pure}} / \sigma_{\text{h3-1}}$, defined as the standard deviation under the pure-recurrent configuration divided by that under the highest attention ratio (25\%). Values greater than 1 indicate that higher attention ratios are associated with lower cross-architecture variance.
48	
49	\subsection{Per-Example Decomposition}\label{ssec:per_example_decomposition}
50	
51	We decompose performance changes at the individual-example level on four benchmarks spanning three task categories: LAMBADA (5{,}153 examples; retrieval), HellaSwag (10{,}042; reasoning), PIQA (1{,}838; reasoning, two-choice), and ARC-Easy (2{,}376; mixed). HellaSwag uses normalized accuracy (acc\_norm) as the per-example correctness criterion. Supplementary analyses on BoolQ (3{,}270) and WinoGrande (1{,}267) appear in Appendix~\ref{app:boolq} and \ref{app:winogrande}. For each architecture, we compare the pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid (25\% attention), which maximizes the recurrent--hybrid contrast; \S\ref{ssec:per_example} confirms that $\alpha$ is stable across intermediate ratios (variation $
…[truncated 3749 chars]

_source line 1696 · 2026-05-25T16:45:33.168823+00:00_

60	
61	To measure cross-architecture overlap, we compute the pairwise Jaccard similarity $J = |A \cap B| / |A \cup B|$ between the helped sets of two architectures and, separately, between their hurt sets. $J = 1$ indicates identical sets; $J = 0$ indicates no overlap. On $K$-choice tasks, binarization collapses $K{-}1$ wrong answers into a single ``incorrect'' category, potentially counting architectures as agreeing (both wrong) when they select different wrong answers. This conservative bias strengthens the multiplicity claim: true divergence may exceed what binary Jaccard captures. Appendix~\ref{app:continuous_jaccard} confirms robustness under continuous Jaccard similarity.
62	
63	\subsection{Statistical Methods}\label{ssec:statistical_methods}
64	
65	All tests are two-tailed. Spearman $\rho$ uses exact permutation tests ($5! = 120$ permutations); with $n = 5$, only $|\rho| = 1.0$ achieves $p < .05$ ($p = .017$), so all Spearman-based inferences are descriptive and exploratory. Bonferroni correction spans all 10 benchmarks (adjusted threshold $\alpha / 10 = 0.005$). Bootstrap 95\% bias-corrected and accelerated (BCa) confidence intervals use 10{,}000 resamples; with $n = 3$ architectures, these are heuristic indicators of effect direction. Within-architecture comparisons use Mann-Whitney U; cross-architecture comparisons use Kruskal-Wallis. Attention weight analysis extracts per-layer cosine similarity and Shannon entropy via forward hooks (Appendix~\ref{app:attention_patterns}). The permutation null does not condition on item difficulty; if difficulty-driven correlations inflate observed Jaccard while permutation destroys this structure, the test may be anti-conservative. However, the ${\sim}2.5\times$ observed-to-null ratio (all $z > 5.9$) provides substantial margin against this bias.
66	
67

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	Using the model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid} (\S\ref{ssec:model_zoo}), we evaluate all 30 configurations on 10 benchmarks plus supplementary COPA ($n = 100$) at 1.3B scale unless noted. Results at 340M appear in scale analyses. Recall: h$k$-1 denotes one attention layer per $k{+}1$ layers; h3-1 = 25\% attention.
4	
5	\subsection{Per-Example Divergence}\label{ssec:per_example}
6	
7	Do architectures converge by improving on the same examples? We compare each architecture's pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid on 5{,}153 LAMBADA examples, classifying each as helped, hurt, or unchanged. Although the decomposition is binary, it captures genuine separation in the continuous confidence space (Cohen's $d = 0.73$--$0.92$ between helped and hurt on LAMBADA; cross-benchmark details in Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}). Binarization discards confidence magnitude, so we supplement with continuous confidence margin analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}) and verify that low Jaccard persists under continuous thresholds (Appendix~\ref{app:continuous_jaccard}).
8	
9	Each architecture displays a distinct profile of per-example changes (Figure~\ref{fig:per_example_bars}). GDN loses a net 153 examples (462 helped, 615 hurt), RetNet loses 69 (426/495), and HGRN2 gains 153 (548/395; full counts in Appendix~\ref{app:per_example_counts}). HGRN2 is the only architecture for which adding attention yields a net per-example benefit. Pairwise Jaccard similarity is low: mean $\bar{J} \leq 0.10$, indicating ${\sim}90$\% non-overlap. Overlap significantly exceeds the independence baseline ($J_{\text{null}} \approx 0.05$, all $z > 5.9$, $p < .0001$; Appendix~\ref{app:jaccard_permutation}), plausibly reflecting shared sensitivity to example difficulty. Normalizing by $J_{\max}$, observed overlap reaches only 6--7\% of the achievable range above chance (Appendix~\ref{app:normalized_es}), confirming that low Jaccard cannot be attribute
…[truncated 3114 chars]

(b) Confidence-based routing gains track $\\alpha$: effective when multiplicity is high (LAMBADA) but counterproductive when low (PIQA). 1.3B, h3-1.}\n\\label{fig:alpha_routing}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nEach architecture displays"
}

    "old_string": "Do architectures converge by improving on the same examples? We compare each architecture's pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid on 5{,}153 LAMBADA examples, classifying each as helped, hurt, or unchanged. Binarization discards confidence magnitude, so we supplement with continuous confidence margin analysis (Appendix~\\ref{app:confidence_margin}) and verify that low Jaccard persists under continuous thresholds (Appendix~\\ref{app:continuous_jaccard}).\n\nEach architecture displays",
    "new_string": "Do architectures converge by improving on the same examples? We compare each architecture's pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid on 5{,}153 LAMBADA examples, classifying each as helped, hurt, or unchanged. Although the decomposition is binary, it captures genuine separation in the continuous confidence space (Cohen's $d = 0.73$--$0.92$ between helped and hurt on LAMBADA; cross-benchmark details in Appendix~\\ref{app:cm_cross}). Binarization discards confidence magnitude, so we supplement with continuous confidence margin analysis (Appendix~\\ref{app:confidence_margin}) and verify that low Jaccard persists under continuous thresholds (Appendix~\\ref{app:continuous_jaccard}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_method_overview.pdf}\n\\caption{Experimental framework. For each architecture, we compare the pure-recurrent baseline against its hybrid (+attention) variant, classifying every evaluation example as helped, hurt, or unchanged. Cross-architecture overlap of these per-example effect sets is measured via pairwise Jaccard similarity ($J \\approx 0.10$), revealing architecture-specific sensitivity patterns.}\n\\label{fig:method_overview}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_per_example_bars.pdf}\n\\caption{Per-example effects of adding attention (pure $\\to$ h3-1) on LAMBADA (1.3B). Helped: correct only with attention; hurt: correct only without. HGRN2 shows the only net positive effect; overlap is minimal ($\\bar{J} \\leq 0.10$, all $p < .0001$).}\n\\label{fig:per_example_bars}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.75\\textwidth]{figures/fig3_alpha_routing.pdf}\n\\caption{(a) Per-example ambiguity $\\alpha$ varies $2\\times$ across task types. (b) Confidence-based routing gains track $\\alpha$: effective when multiplicity is high (LAMBADA) but counterproductive when low (PIQA). 1.3B, h3-1.}\n\\label{fig:alpha_routing}\n\\end{figure*}\n\nEach architecture displays"

30	Each architecture displays a distinct profile of per-example changes (Figure~\ref{fig:per_example_bars}). HGRN2 is the only architecture for which adding attention yields a net per-example benefit. Pairwise Jaccard similarity is low: mean $\bar{J} \leq 0.10$, indicating ${\sim}90$\% non-overlap. Overlap significantly exceeds the independence baseline ($J_{\text{null}} \approx 0.05$, all $z > 5.9$, $p < .0001$; Appendix~\ref{app:jaccard_permutation}), plausibly reflecting shared sensitivity to example difficulty. Normalizing by $J_{\max}$, observed overlap reaches only 6--7\% of the achievable range above chance (Appendix~\ref{app:normalized_es}), confirming that low Jaccard cannot be attributed to set-size constraints.

41	All evaluations use lm-evaluation-harness v0.4.x \citep{gao2024lmeval} with default prompts and greedy decoding; accuracy serves as the primary metric for all benchmarks. Bootstrap analyses use random seed 42 throughout.
42	
43	\subsection{Convergence Metrics}\label{ssec:convergence_metrics}
44	
45	We quantify cross-architecture agreement as a function of attention ratio. For each benchmark and attention ratio, we compute the cross-architecture standard deviation $\sigma$ over the three architectures' accuracy scores. To measure the relationship between attention and convergence, we compute the Spearman rank correlation $\rho$ between attention ratio rank and $\sigma$ across the five attention configurations ($n = 5$). A negative $\rho$ indicates that higher attention ratios are associated with lower cross-architecture variance.
46	
47	We also report the convergence ratio $\sigma_{\text{pure}} / \sigma_{\text{h3-1}}$, defined as the standard deviation under the pure-recurrent configuration divided by that under the highest attention ratio (25\%). Values greater than 1 indicate that higher attention ratios are associated with lower cross-architecture variance.
48	
49	\subsection{Per-Example Decomposition}\label{ssec:per_example_decomposition}
50	
51	We decompose performance changes at the individual-example level on four benchmarks spanning three task categories: LAMBADA (5{,}153 examples; retrieval), HellaSwag (10{,}042; reasoning), PIQA (1{,}838; reasoning, two-choice), and ARC-Easy (2{,}376; mixed). HellaSwag uses normalized accuracy (acc\_norm) as the per-example correctness criterion. Supplementary analyses on BoolQ (3{,}270) and WinoGrande (1{,}267) appear in Appendix~\ref{app:boolq} and \ref{app:winogrande}. For each architecture, we compare the pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid (25\% attention), which maximizes the recurrent--hybrid contrast; \S\ref{ssec:per_example} confirms that $\alpha$ is stable across intermediate ratios (variation $

37	\paragraph{Multiplicity variation.} Extending to HellaSwag ($N = 10{,}042$) and PIQA ($N = 1{,}838$) reveals that multiplicity varies systematically across tasks (Table~\ref{tab:multiplicity}). Ambiguity $\alpha$ \citep{marx2020predictive} ranges from 0.14 (PIQA) through 0.15 (HellaSwag) and 0.21 (ARC-Easy; Appendix~\ref{app:arceasy}) to 0.30 (LAMBADA). Format does not drive this ordering: HellaSwag (four-way) and PIQA (two-choice) produce nearly identical $\alpha$ despite different answer-space sizes ($\rho = -0.60$, $p = .24$ with task accuracy). This suggests multiplicity is associated with cognitive demands rather than evaluation format, though the confound cannot be fully resolved (Limitations). Bootstrap 95\% CIs confirm the ordering (all non-overlapping except PIQA--HellaSwag; Table~\ref{tab:arceasy_ambiguity}). Pairwise Jaccard remains uniformly low ($\bar{J} \leq 0.10$, all $p < .01$), indicating architectures affect different examples regardless of how many are affected. Within each benchmark, $\alpha$ is stable across attention ratios (variation $< 0.03$) but differs $2\times$ across task types, confirming that multiplicity magnitude depends on task, not attention ratio \citep[cf.][]{gorbett2026crossmodel}.

_source line 1768 · 2026-05-25T16:55:58.414513+00:00_

70	\subsection{Statistical Methods}\label{ssec:statistical_methods}
71	
72	All tests are two-tailed. Spearman $\rho$ uses exact permutation tests ($5! = 120$ permutations); with $n = 5$, only $|\rho| = 1.0$ achieves $p < .05$ ($p = .017$), so all Spearman-based inferences are descriptive and exploratory. Bonferroni correction spans all 10 benchmarks (adjusted threshold $\alpha / 10 = 0.005$). Bootstrap 95\% bias-corrected and accelerated (BCa) confidence intervals use 10{,}000 resamples; with $n = 3$ architectures, these are heuristic indicators of effect direction. Within-architecture comparisons use Mann-Whitney U; cross-architecture comparisons use Kruskal-Wallis. Attention weight analysis extracts per-layer cosine similarity and Shannon entropy via forward hooks (Appendix~\ref{app:attention_patterns}). The permutation null does not condition on item difficulty; if difficulty-driven correlations inflate observed Jaccard while permutation destroys this structure, the test may be anti-conservative. However, the ${\sim}2.5\times$ observed-to-null ratio (all $z > 5.9$) provides substantial margin against this bias.
73	
74	
75

_source line 1793 · 2026-05-25T17:08:49.286235+00:00_

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	Using the model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid} (\S\ref{ssec:model_zoo}), we evaluate all 30 configurations on 10 benchmarks plus supplementary COPA ($n = 100$) at 1.3B scale unless noted. Results at 340M appear in scale analyses. Recall: h$k$-1 denotes one attention layer per $k{+}1$ layers; h3-1 = 25\% attention.
4	
5	\subsection{Per-Example Divergence}\label{ssec:per_example}
6	
7	Do architectures converge by improving on the same examples? We compare each architecture's pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid on 5{,}153 LAMBADA examples, classifying each as helped, hurt, or unchanged. Although the decomposition is binary, it captures genuine separation in the continuous confidence space (Cohen's $d = 0.73$--$0.92$ between helped and hurt on LAMBADA; cross-benchmark details in Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}). Binarization discards confidence magnitude, so we supplement with continuous confidence margin analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}) and verify that low Jaccard persists under continuous thresholds (Appendix~\ref{app:continuous_jaccard}).
8	
9	\begin{figure*}[t]
10	\centering
11	\includegraphics[width=0.75\textwidth]{figures/fig3_alpha_routing.pdf}
12	\caption{(a) Per-example ambiguity $\alpha$ varies $2\times$ across task types. (b) Confidence-based routing gains track $\alpha$: effective when multiplicity is high (LAMBADA) but counterproductive when low (PIQA). 1.3B, h3-1.}
13	\label{fig:alpha_routing}
14	\end{figure*}
15	
16	\begin{figure}[t]
17	\centering
18	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jaccard_hierarchy.pdf}
19	\caption{Jaccard hierarchy on LAMBADA (1.3B). Cross-architecture overlap ($\bar{J} \approx 0.09$) is $2\times$ the independence baseline but only one-third of within-architecture overlap ($\bar{J} \approx 0.33$). Pythia 70M within-seed Jaccard (different scale) falls at a comparable level. Error bars show the helped--hurt range.}
20	\label{fig:jaccard_hierarchy}

[Audit 回复]:
[PAPER AUDIT] entrainment_gated_ft 论文质量审计报告

━━━ 确定性检查 ━━━
编译: ✅
页数: 21
源码: 2 critical, 100 warning

━━━ 内容结构 ━━━
- [info] [叙事一致性] Abstract → Intro → Method → Experiments → Conclusion 主线一致：核心问题（aggregate equivalence ≠ behavioral equivalence）、方法（per-example decomposition via controlled model zoo）、三条贡献均在各章节对齐。未检测到叙事漂移。
- [critical] [叙事一致性] method.tex:72 声称 "the ~2.5× observed-to-null ratio (all z > 5.9)"，但 Abstract (main.tex:38) 正确写为 "roughly twice the independence baseline"。根据论文自身数据（Appendix Table 7/tab:jaccard_perm）：mean J_obs/J_null = (0.100/0.048, 0.093/0.050) ≈ 1.9–2.1×，不是 2.5×。Abstract 和 Method 对同一指标给出了矛盾的描述（"roughly twice" vs "~2.5×"），且 Method 的 "~2.5×" 比实际数据高 ~25%。审稿人对照 Table 7 就能发现。
- [warning] [叙事一致性] Abstract (main.tex:38) "Jaccard ≈ 0.10 ... all p < .01 across five architectures (three primary, two validation)" 的括号结构容易让读者将 "Jaccard ≈ 0.10" 同时应用于 5 架构，但 5 架构验证（340M, Appendix H）mean J_h ≈ 0.122（最高 0.134），高于 0.10。主文 §4.3 正确写为 "J ≤ 0.14"。建议 Abstract 改为 "Jaccard ≤ 0.14" 或将 "≈ 0.10" 限定为 3 架构结果。
- [info] [章节结构] Abstract 回答了四个问题（问题/方法/结果/意义），~159 words，符合 EMNLP ≤ 200 words 要求。
- [info] [章节结构] Introduction 结构清晰：Hook → Context → Gap → This paper → Central finding → Contribution list。
- [info] [章节结构] Related Work 按三个主题组织（Hybrid Architectures / Linear Recurrent Models / Underspecification & Multiplicity），有归纳比较而非论文堆砌。
- [info] [章节结构] Conclusion 非常简短（6 行），仅总结发现。无 Discussion 节。对于 ACL/EMNLP 论文可接受，但审稿人可能期望更多 implications/future directions 的讨论。
- [info] [贡献-范围] 三条 Contribution 均有对应 section/table 支撑：C1→§4.1+Table 3; C2→§4.2+Figure 1; C3→§4.1 confidence paragraph + Appendix D/E。未检测到孤儿章节。
- [info] [贡献-范围] Title 范围 "Hybrid Linear Recurrent Models" 与实验覆盖的 3+2 个线性递归架构对齐。
- [critical] [Claim 强度] method.tex:72 "~2.5× observed-to-null ratio" 数值不匹配论文自身数据（实际 ~2.0×）。详见叙事一致性条目。
- [info] [Claim 强度] "significantly exceeds the independence baseline" (experiments.tex:23) 有置换检验支撑 (z > 5.9, p < .0001)。合规。
- [info] [Claim 强度] "significantly negative" (experiments.tex:28, PIQA routing -0.6pp) 有 p = .006 支撑。合规。
- [info] [Claim 强度] 无 "state-of-the-art" 或 "novel" 等过强声称。Claim 整体谨慎，大量使用 hedging ("appears to stabilize", "partially predict", "suggestive")。
- [warning] [实验严谨性] 主结果表 (Table 3/tab:multiplicity) 无 error bars 或 CI。α 的 BCa bootstrap CI 仅在 Appendix Table 6 中给出，Jaccard 的 CI 未显式报告。建议在 Table 3 增加 CI 列或脚注引用 Appendix CI。
- [info] [实验严谨性] 单次训练已在 Limitations 中明确声明。置换检验（10,000 permutations）提供统计可信度。5 架构验证增强外部有效性。Ablation（GDN 排除、连续 Jaccard、跨 scale）覆盖充分。
- [info] [可复现性] GPU 型号（RTX PRO 6000）、计算量（~5 GPU-hours inference）、评估框架（lm-eval-harness v0.4.x）、随机种子（42）、代码 URL 均已披露。可复现性信息完备。
- [info] [符号] α（ambiguity）、J（Jaccard）、σ（cross-arch std）、ρ（Spearman）、hk-1 notation 全文一致，首次出现处均有定义。
- [info] [符号] "helped/hurt/unchanged" 在 §3.4 定义，全文一致使用。
- [info] [逻辑流] §4.1→§4.2 有明确过渡句 "Having established that multiplicity varies by task type, we now ask whether it changes with the amount of attention."
- [info] [逻辑流] §4.2 内含两种解释（fingerprint vs convergence），兼具 Discussion 功能。
- [info] [Limitations] 4 段覆盖 4 个 distinct limitations：单次训练、format confound、覆盖范围（架构/benchmark/scale）、仅 pretrained+greedy decoding。~155 words，实质性强。
- [warning] [去AI] related_work.tex:7 LaTeX em-dash (---): "gated-linear RNNs---including RetNet and HGRN---can all be cast as..." → 典型解释性 em-dash，AI 写作标志。建议改为括号或逗号。
- [warning] [去AI] appendix.tex:434 "findings---within-architecture predictability coexists with..." → 解释性 em-dash。
- [warning] [去AI] appendix.tex:545 "boundary---the confidence level below which..." → 解释性 em-dash。
- [warning] [去AI] appendix.tex:653 "multiplicity---which architectures affect which examples---is consistent..." → 双 em-dash包夹插入语。

- [info] [审稿规则] 正文 8 页限制：基于内容量估算（intro ~1.5p + related ~0.75p + method ~2p + experiments ~3p + conclusion ~0.25p + limitations ~0.25p ≈ 7.75p），应在限制内。已编译为 21 页（含 appendix + references），正文页数需最终编译确认。

- [warning] [page 5] Figure 3 的 Helped(绿)/Hurt(红) 配色为经典红绿对比，对色觉障碍读者（protanopia/deuteranopia）不友好。建议改用 Blue+Orange 或 Okabe-Ito 色板，并辅以不同 hatch pattern 区分
- [info] [page 1] Abstract 篇幅从视觉估计接近 200 words 上限（EMNLP 2026 硬约束），需用精确工具计数确认是否超限
- [info] [page 6] Figure 5(a) 的条形图同样使用了红色系与绿色系区分类别（Reasoning vs Mixed vs Minimal），与 Figure 3 存在相同的色盲可及性隐患
- [info] [page 6] Figure 6 散点图较小（单栏宽度），轴标签和数据点注释字号偏小，打印后可能阅读吃力
- [info] [page 7] Figure 7 热力图单元格内数值字号较小（约为正文 60-70%），在黑白打印时蓝色渐变区分度可能不足
- [info] [pages 14-20] Appendix 极度表格密集（部分页面含 4-5 个表格），表间几乎无连接文字，导航困难。建议在关键表前添加一句话说明其目的
- [info] [page 5] Figure 4 热力图仅使用蓝色单色渐变；当数值差异较小时（如 0.00-0.06 范围），颜色区分度有限，建议标注数值（已做）的同时考虑加大色阶跨度

━━━ 总计 ━━━

===== 行动指引 =====

1. Critical 必须修：叙事漂移→统一全文叙事，占位符→立即清理，数字不一致→核对 registry，内部信息泄露→替换为描述性名称
2. Warning 建议修：去 AI 风格→批量修改，排版问题→调整 float/空白
3. Info 仅供参考

叙事不一致的论文比有 typo 的论文更难被接收。先统一叙事，再处理其他问题。

审阅完毕后，用 director_reply_to_user 向 A 群汇报审计结论和行动计划。

_source line 1814 · 2026-05-25T17:19:32.805516+00:00_

70	\subsection{Statistical Methods}\label{ssec:statistical_methods}
71	
72	All tests are two-tailed. Spearman $\rho$ uses exact permutation tests ($5! = 120$ permutations); with $n = 5$, only $|\rho| = 1.0$ achieves $p < .05$ ($p = .017$), so all Spearman-based inferences are descriptive and exploratory. Bonferroni correction spans all 10 benchmarks (adjusted threshold $\alpha / 10 = 0.005$). Bootstrap 95\% bias-corrected and accelerated (BCa) confidence intervals use 10{,}000 resamples; with $n = 3$ architectures, these are heuristic indicators of effect direction. Within-architecture comparisons use Mann-Whitney U; cross-architecture comparisons use Kruskal-Wallis. Attention weight analysis extracts per-layer cosine similarity and Shannon entropy via forward hooks (Appendix~\ref{app:attention_patterns}). The permutation null does not condition on item difficulty; if difficulty-driven correlations inflate observed Jaccard while permutation destroys this structure, the test may be anti-conservative. However, the ${\sim}2.5\times$ observed-to-null ratio (all $z > 5.9$) provides substantial margin against this bias.
73	
74	\begin{figure}[t]
75	\centering
76	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_per_example_bars.pdf}
77	\caption{Per-example effects of adding attention (pure $\to$ h3-1) on LAMBADA (1.3B). Helped: correct only with attention; hurt: correct only without. HGRN2 shows the only net positive effect; overlap is minimal ($\bar{J} \leq 0.10$, all $p < .0001$).}
78	\label{fig:per_example_bars}
79	\end{figure}

_source line 1818 · 2026-05-25T17:19:42.469191+00:00_

36	
37	\begin{abstract}
38	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\approx 0.10$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures (three primary, two validation)). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\% attention and remains flat across higher ratios, suggesting that minimal attention may establish which examples each architecture affects. \citet{yang2026benchmark} document analogous per-item disagreement (16--66\%) among frontier large language models (LLMs) despite comparable aggregate scores; our within-family design isolates backbone variation from training-regime differences. Confidence margins partially predict affected examples (area under the ROC curve [AUC] 0.70--0.74). Low cross-architecture overlap implies substantial complementarity, though routing-based exploitation remains task-dependent\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/entrainment_gated_ft-9444/}.}.
39	\end{abstract}
40

5	
6	\paragraph{Linear Recurrent Models.}
7	Our three recurrent backbones represent distinct gating strategies within the linear recurrence family: RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet} uses multi-scale exponential decay, HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} applies gated state expansion, and GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet} combines gated updates with delta rules, extending GLA \citep{yang2024gla} and DeltaNet \citep{yang2024deltanet}. These descend from S4 \citep{gu2022s4} and Mamba \citep{gu2024mamba}, which introduced structured state spaces and input-dependent selection, and connect to linear attention \citep{katharopoulos2020transformers} through the state-space--attention duality of Mamba-2. xLSTM \citep{beck2024xlstm} revives the LSTM lineage with exponential gating and matrix memory, offering an alternative approach at scale. \citet{zimerman2025unified} show that modern gated-linear RNNs---including RetNet and HGRN---can all be cast as implicit causal self-attention, providing a unifying perspective on the family. \citet{arora2024based} identify a recall-throughput tradeoff in linear models that motivates hybrid designs.
8	
9	\paragraph{Underspecification and Predictive Multiplicity.}

_source line 1842 · 2026-05-25T17:20:29.054165+00:00_

543	\paragraph{Normality verification.} Shapiro-Wilk tests reject normality for all six architecture--set combinations (all $W < 0.98$, $p < 10^{-6}$), but the practical departure varies by group. Disruption-set $|\log p|$ values show mild skewness (0.53--0.77, $W \in [0.955, 0.973]$), closely approximating a normal distribution at the sample sizes involved. Rescue-set values are moderately right-skewed (skewness 1.33--1.41, $W \in [0.899, 0.920]$), following an approximately log-normal distribution. Because the skew is one-sided, parametric AUC estimates (0.70--0.74 in the main text) are conservative lower bounds; non-parametric empirical AUC from the same data yields 0.73--0.77.
544	
545	Despite similar within-architecture AUC, the optimal prediction thresholds differ across architectures for disruption but not rescue (Table~\ref{tab:threshold_gap}). Rescue thresholds cluster tightly (range 0.03 in $|\log p|$), indicating that the rescuability boundary---the confidence level below which pure-wrong examples are likely to be corrected by attention---is architecture-invariant. Disruption thresholds span a 47\% relative range (GDN: 0.70, RetNet: 1.00, HGRN2: 1.14), meaning that the same confidence level on a pure-correct example may predict vulnerability in one architecture but safety in another. This asymmetry constitutes a second dimension of multiplicity beyond per-example disagreement: all three architectures agree on which wrong answers are rescuable, but disagree on which correct answers are vulnerable to disruption.
546	
547	\begin{table}[ht]

651	\subsection{Cross-Benchmark Scale Comparison}
652	
653	Table~\ref{tab:340m_multi_bench} extends the scale comparison to all four main benchmarks. Two patterns emerge. First, $\alpha$ is systematically higher at 340M across all benchmarks, with the largest gap on ARC-Easy ($+$8.4pp) and the smallest on LAMBADA ($+$0.6pp). Second, pairwise Jaccard remains stable across scales ($\bar{J}_h = 0.08$--$0.10$ at both scales); three-architecture results are stable across both 340M and 1.3B scales (Jaccard $\approx$ 0.08--0.10), providing indirect support for consistency across these two scales. This suggests that the qualitative pattern of per-example multiplicity---which architectures affect which examples---is consistent across the two scales examined, even as its magnitude increases at smaller scale. Notably, the $\alpha$ inflation does not track accuracy loss: HellaSwag has the largest accuracy gap between scales yet the smallest $\Delta\alpha$ ($+$1.9pp), while ARC-Easy has a smaller accuracy gap but the largest $\Delta\alpha$ ($+$8.4pp), arguing against accuracy as the primary driver of scale-dependent multiplicity.
654

[WRITE HANDOFF] 请立即写你的 handoff。

**格式硬约束**:
- 整条回复**只能**是 &lt;handoff>...&lt;/handoff> 标签包裹
- 禁止在标签外写任何寒暄 / 说明 / 确认（"好的"/"我开始写"/"写完了"等）

**必须覆盖以下 5 个 section（缺一不可，无内容写 N/A）**:

### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
  本项目特定的不可违反约束（GPU 设备号、服务器名、账号、路径限制等）。
  写本项目特定的，不要抄 CLAUDE.md 通用 L1 红线。
  下一个"你"第一眼看到的就是这里，写错了会被 Director CRITICAL。

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
  - 服务器名 + 工作目录 + Python 路径 + conda env
  - 模型路径 / 数据集路径 / wandb config / HF cache
  - 活跃进程：PID + tmux session + cron 任务 ID
  写一次就稳定，下一个"你"不用再 SSH 探查。

### 3. 📊 当前状态
  - 你刚做完什么 / 正在做什么
  - registry 里 RUNNING/PREPARING 的实验列表 + 一句话摘要
  - 等待中的 Worker 回调 / Cron / Director 决策

### 4. 📋 待办（条件触发式）
  格式 "条件 X 满足 → 动作 Y"。例如:
  - "Falcon-H1R 下载完成 → sed 修 MODEL_PATH → dry-run → 提交"
  - "pilot_011 出 GC4 结果 → ≥2pp 转架构 narrative，<2pp 写 null-result"

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
  - 项目核心结论 / 决策链（D001..）
  - 下一个"你"最容易判断错的事 / 容易踩的坑

**写作原则**：
- 第 1 节必须是项目特定的（不是通用 L1 红线）
- 第 2 节让下一轮 BOOT 后能立即开工，避免重新 SSH 探查
- 简洁 > 完整：每节 5-10 行足够，长 handoff 不等于好 handoff

示例格式:
&lt;handoff>
### 1. 🔴 硬约束 / 红线
- 本项目只能用 cuda:4 / cuda:5（之前两次违规 cuda:0 被 Director CRITICAL）
- submit_training_job 不支持 shell builtin（source/conda activate 全不行）

### 2. 🖥️ 基础设施快照
- 服务器：westd-8x6000，工作目录 /root/autodl-tmp/hybrid_reasoning_phases
- Python：/root/miniconda3/bin/python（绝对路径，不走 conda activate）
- 模型：/root/autodl-tmp/qwen35_4b_direct
- 活跃：pilot_011 (PID 203221, tmux=pilot_011, cuda:4)

### 3. 📊 当前状态
我刚完成 pilot_010 分析，正在等 pilot_011 (Falcon-H1R) CoT 生成完成（6/50）。
registry: pilot_011 RUNNING；其他全 DONE。

### 4. 📋 待办
- pilot_011 cron 回调 → spawn worker 跑 probe + GC4 计算
- GC4 ≥2pp → 转架构 narrative；<2pp → null-result paper outline 已就绪

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
Falcon-H1R 是最后一个数据点。已知 4B/9B GC4 都 FAIL。
易错：不要把 GC4-par (Falcon parallel) 和 GC4-seq (Qwen sequential) 直接对比。
&lt;/handoff>

### 3. 📊 当前状态
刚完成一轮排版微调，已通知用户：
- Table 3 从双栏 `table*` 改为单栏 `table`，缩小字体+缩写分类名，编译通过

### 5. 🎯 战略上下文
- 项目核心发现：hybrid linear recurrent 架构在 aggregate benchmark 上等价，但 per-example 层面存在 structured predictive multiplicity（Jaccard ≤ 0.14），multiplicity 随 task type 变化（α=0.14–0.30）而非 attention ratio
- 论文 framing 已稳定：analysis paper，不是 method paper。Core claims 全部有实验支撑
- 今天是 ARR DDL，用户在做最后的排版打磨，不涉及内容变更
- 易错点：修改 LaTeX 浮动体位置时，单栏 figure 和双栏 figure* 走不同的 float queue，改一个图的位置可能连锁影响其他图的页面分布——每次改动后必须全文编译+视觉检查所有页面
